\displaystyle g(O):=\begin{cases}\Tr(O)^{2}&\text{when }O\geq 0,\\
\Tr(O^{2})&\text{when }\Tr(O)=0.\end{cases}

(S47)

Lemma S6
.

Suppose integer
k\geq 1
,
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
,
and
O
is an observable on
\mathcal{H}
with
O\geq 0
or
\Tr(O)=0
.
Then

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+2}}\Big|\!\Tr\!\big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)R_{\pi}\big]\Big|\leq g(O)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|\leq\max_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]}}\right|\leq\sqrt{g(O)}.

(S48)

Proof of Lemma
S6
.

Note that

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+2}}\Big|\!\Tr\!\big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)R_{\pi}\big]\Big|\leq\max_{i,j|0\leq i+j\leq k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{i}O\rho^{j})}}\right|,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{i})\Tr(O\rho^{j})}}\right|}}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr(O)^{2},\Tr(O^{2})}}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}g(O),

(S49)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|\leq\max_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]}}\right|\leq\max_{i|0\leq i\leq k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{i})}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr(O),\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2})}}}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\sqrt{g(O)},

(S50)

where
(a)
and
(c)
follow from Lemma
S7
below;

(b)
and
(d)
hold because
\Tr(O^{2})=\|O\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|O\|_{1}^{2}=\Tr(O)^{2}
when
O\geq 0
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S7
.

Suppose integers
k,l\geq 1
,
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
,
and
O
is an Hermitian observable on
\mathcal{H}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{k})}}\right|\leq\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2})},\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O^{2}\rho^{k})}}\right|\leq\Tr(O^{2}),\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{k}O\rho^{l})}}\right|\leq\Tr(O^{2}).

(S51)

Proof of Lemma
S7
.

Equation (
S51
) can be derived as follows:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{k})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|O\|\leq\|O\|_{2}=\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2})},

(S52)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O^{2}\rho^{k})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|O^{2}\|\leq\|O^{2}\|_{1}=\Tr(O^{2}),

(S53)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\rho^{k}O\rho^{l})}}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|O\rho^{k}\|_{2}\|O\rho^{l}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2}\rho^{2k})}\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2}\rho^{2l})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\Tr(O^{2}),

(S54)

where
(a)
follows from Hölder’s inequality and
(b)
follows from Eq. (
S53
).
∎

B
Expectation and variance of our estimator
\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)

The statistical fluctuation of our estimator
\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)
[see Eqs. (
4
) and (
S46
)] comes from:
(i) the randomness in the selection of the random unitary
U
, and
(ii) the randomness in quantum measurement outcomes
\mathbf{b}_{U}=\{\hat{b}_{1},\dots,\hat{b}_{N_{M}}\}
.
Taking these two sources of randomness into account, the expectation and variance of
\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)
are
clarified by the following two lemmas.

Lemma S8
.

Suppose
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
(k+1)
-design ensemble
\mathcal{E}_{k+1}
, then

{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]=\xi_{k}^{O}
for any observable
O
.

Proof of Lemma
S8
.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)\Big|U}}\right)=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}=b\,|U}}\right)\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\,\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\langle b|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{k}\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle}}\right]=\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)\Phi_{\mathcal{E}_{k+1}}\big(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|^{\otimes{(k+1)}}\big)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(k+1)}\Big]=\frac{1}{(k+1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]=\xi_{k}^{O}.

(S55)

This confirms Lemma
S8
, implying that
\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)
is an unbiased estimator of
\xi_{k}^{O}
.
∎

Lemma S9
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer independent of the system dimension
d
,

O
is an Hermitian observable satisfying
O\geq 0
or
\Tr(O)=0
,
and
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
2(k+1)
-design ensemble
\mathcal{E}_{2k+2}
. Then

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(O)\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right),

(S56)

where
g(O)
is defined in Eq.
(
S47
)
.

Proof of Lemma
S9
.

The variance of
\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)
reads

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right)^{2}}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]^{2}=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right)^{2}\Big|U}}\right]-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}}}\right)^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leq N_{M}\\
1\leq j_{1}<\dots<j_{k}\leq N_{M}\end{subarray}}\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}.

(S57)

where the second equality follows from Lemma
S8
, and the function
f_{1}
is defined as

\displaystyle f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O):=\textbf{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}}}\right\}\textbf{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{b}_{j_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{j_{k}}}}\right\}\langle\hat{b}_{i_{1}}|UOU^{{\dagger}}|\hat{b}_{i_{1}}\rangle\langle\hat{b}_{j_{1}}|UOU^{{\dagger}}|\hat{b}_{j_{1}}\rangle.

(S58)

To calculate
\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)
, we consider two cases below.
Let
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]
be the number of common elements shared by
(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})
and
(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
.
First, if
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l
with
l\in\{0,1,\dots,k-1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\langle b|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{k+l}\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes(k+l)}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Phi_{\mathcal{E}_{2k+2}}\big(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|^{\otimes(k+l+2)}\big)}}\right]=\frac{d}{\kappa_{k+l+2}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes(k+l)}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(k+l+2)}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{(k+l+2)!\,\kappa_{k+l+2}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k+l+2}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes{(k+l)}}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{k+l+1}}}}\right),

(S59)

where the last equality follows from Lemma
S6
.

Second, if
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(

\displaystyle f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\sum_{b,b^{\prime}=0}^{d-1}\langle b|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{k}\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle\langle b^{\prime}|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b^{\prime}\rangle^{k}\langle b^{\prime}|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\sum_{b=0}^{d-1}\langle b|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{2k}\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{2}}_{(*1)}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\sum_{b\neq b^{\prime}}\langle b|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle^{k}\langle b|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b\rangle\langle b^{\prime}|U\rho U^{{\dagger}}|b^{\prime}\rangle^{k}\langle b^{\prime}|UOU^{{\dagger}}|b^{\prime}\rangle}_{(*2)}.

(S60)

By using an argument similar to that in Eq. (
B
), we can derive that
(*1)=\mathcal{O}\big[d^{-2k-1}g(O)\big]
.
For the term
(*2)
, we have

\displaystyle(*2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\neq b^{\prime}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b^{\prime}}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}}}\right)\Phi_{\mathcal{E}_{2k+2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\sum_{b\neq b^{\prime}}\sum_{\tau,\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{2k+2}}\mathrm{Wg}_{\pi,\tau}^{(2k+2,d)}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b^{\prime}}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}}}\right)R_{\tau}}}\right]\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\sum_{b\neq b^{\prime}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{2k+2}}\sum_{\,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\mathrm{Wg}_{\pi,(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}^{(2k+2,d)}\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}\Big]

\displaystyle=d(d-1)\times\underbrace{\sum_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\mathrm{Wg}_{(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}^{(2k+2,d)}\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\Big]}_{(*3)}

\displaystyle\quad\ +d(d-1)\times\underbrace{\sum_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{2k+2}\\
\pi\neq(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Wg}_{\pi,(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}^{(2k+2,d)}\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}\Big]}_{(*4)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(k+1)!\,\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right]^{2}}{d^{2k}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{2k+1}}}}\right).

(S61)

Here,

(a)
follows from Eq. (
S5
);

(b)
holds because
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}^{\top}}}\right]=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]
and

\Tr\!\big[\big(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}\!\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b^{\prime}}}\right|^{\otimes(k+1)}\big)R_{\tau}\big]=1
if
\tau=(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
for some
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}
, and 0 otherwise;

(c)
follows from the following relations:

\displaystyle(*3)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\sum_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[d^{-2k-2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{-2k-3}}}\right)}}\right]\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\tau_{1}}\Big]\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\tau_{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2k+2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{\tau_{1}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\tau_{1}}\Big]}}\right\}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{d^{2k+3}}}}\right)\max_{\tau_{1}\in\mathrm{S}_{k+1}}\Big|\Tr\!\big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\otimes O}}\right)R_{\tau_{1}}\big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{d^{2k+2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(k+1)!\,\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right]^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{2k+3}}}}\right),

(S62)

\displaystyle(*4)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{d^{2k+3}}}}\right)\cdot\max_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{2k+2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes 2k}\otimes O^{\otimes 2}}}\right)R_{\pi}\Big]}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g)}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{2k+3}}}}\right),

(S63)

where
(d)
and
(f)
follows from Eq. (
S6
);

(e)
and
(g)
follows from Lemma
S6
.
In summary, we have

\displaystyle\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)=(*1)+(*2)\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(k+1)!\,\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right]^{2}}{d^{2k}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{2k+1}}}}\right)

(S64)

when
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=0
.

The variance in Eq. (
B
) is then upper bounded by

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}}}\right)^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}<i_{2}<\dots<i_{k},j_{1}<j_{2}<\dots<j_{k},\\
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l\end{subarray}}\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}}}\right)^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\binom{k+l}{k}\binom{k}{l}\,\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(f_{1}(\hat{b}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{i_{k}},\hat{b}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{b}_{j_{k}},U,O)\Big|U\Big)\Big|_{{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{d}}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\,\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\binom{k+l}{k}\binom{k}{l}\,\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{k+l+1}}}}\right)+\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}\bigg[\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((k+1)!\,\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}}{d^{2k}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d^{2k+1}}}}\right)\bigg]}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\mathcal{O}\Big[d^{k-1-l}g(O)\Big]+\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{(k+1)!\kappa_{k+1}}{d^{k+1}}}}\right]^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d}}}\right)}}\right],

(S65)

where the inequality follows from Eqs. (
B
) and (
S64
).

Note that

\displaystyle\frac{(k+1)!\kappa_{k+1}}{d^{k+1}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{k}{d}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{k-1}{d}}}\right)\cdots\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{d}}}\right)=1+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{-1}}}\right)

(S66)

and

\displaystyle\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-k}{N_{M}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-k-1}{N_{M}-1}}}\right)\cdots\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-2k+1}{N_{M}-k+1}}}\right)\leq 1.

(S67)

By plugging these into Eq. (
B
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{k-l-1}g(O)}{N_{M}^{k-l}}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[1+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{-1}}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(O)}{d}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(O)\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2},

(S68)

where we used the relation
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}\leq g(O)
(from Lemma
S6
) in the last line.
This confirms Lemma
S9
.
∎

C
Proof of Theorem
1
in the main text

Theorem 5

(Formal version of Theorem
1
)
.

Suppose
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
,
O
is an observable on
\mathcal{H}
with
\|O\|\leq 1
, and
t>1
is a constant integer independent of
O
and the system dimension
d
.
Let
\mathfrak{B}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr(O_{0}^{2}),1}}\right\}
.
For any
0<\epsilon<1
, the CBNE protocol (given in Algorithm
1
of the End Matter) can return
\epsilon
-additive error estimates for all
p_{2},\dots,p_{t}
and
\Tr(O\rho),\dots,\Tr(O\rho^{t})
with high probability, provided that:
(i)
the random unitaries are sampled from a
2(t+1)
-design ensemble,

\displaystyle(ii)\ N_{U}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1,c_{1}\,\Xi_{1}}}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad(iii)\ N_{M}\geq c_{2}\,d\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Xi_{1}^{1/t},1}}\right\}.

(S69)

Here,
c_{1},c_{2}>0
are some constants independent of
\mathfrak{B},d
and
\epsilon
, and
\Xi_{1}
is a shorthand for
\Xi_{1}(d,\mathfrak{B},\epsilon)=\mathfrak{B}/(d\epsilon^{2})
.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Let
0<\delta<1
be a constant.
According to the union bound and Lemma
S10
below, in order to estimate all
p_{2},\dots,p_{t}
and

\Tr(O\rho),\dots,\Tr(O\rho^{t})
within
\epsilon
additive error and failure probability at most
\delta
, it
suffices to ensure that the following relations hold for all
k=1,2,\dots,t
:

\displaystyle(a)\ \Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{p}_{k}-p_{k}}}\right|>c\epsilon}}\right\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2t}\quad\text{and}\quad(b)\ \Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}-\xi_{k}^{O_{0}}}}\right|>c\epsilon}}\right\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2t},

(S70)

where
O_{0}
is the traceless part of
O
, and
0<c<1
is some constant factor independent of
O
,
\epsilon
and
d
.

According to Theorem
4
, the relation
(a)
in Eq. (
S70
)
holds provided that the conditions in Theorem
5
are satisfied.
We therefore focus on analyzing the relation
(b)
.
By Lemma
S8
,
\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}=\frac{1}{N_{U}}\sum_{s=1}^{N_{U}}\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U_{\!s}}(O_{0})
is an unbiased estimator for
\xi_{k}^{O_{0}}
.
Then Chebyshev’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}-\xi_{k}^{O_{0}}}}\right|>c\epsilon}}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{c^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}}}\right]=\frac{1}{c^{2}\epsilon^{2}N_{U}}\Var\!\Big[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O_{0})\Big]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\Tr(O_{0}^{2})}{\epsilon^{2}N_{U}}\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right),

(S71)

where the last equality follows from Lemma
S9
.
Hence, to make the relation
(b)
in Eq. (
S70
) hold, it is sufficient to take

\displaystyle\frac{\Tr(O_{0}^{2})}{\epsilon^{2}N_{U}d}\leq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\Tr(O_{0}^{2})d^{k-1}}{\epsilon^{2}N_{U}N_{\!M}^{k}}\leq 1,

(S72)

where we omit constant coefficients, including
\delta
and
t
.
It is straightforward to verify that these two conditions are satisfied by choosing
N_{U}
and
N_{M}
as in Theorem
5
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S10
.

Suppose
t\geq 1
is a constant integer and
O
is an Hermitian observable with
\|O\|\leq 1
.
For any
0<\eta<1
, if the estimates
\{\hat{p}_{k}\}_{k=2}^{t}
and
\{\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}\}_{k=1}^{t}
satisfy

\displaystyle|\hat{p}_{k}-p_{k}|\leq\eta\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}-\xi_{k}^{O_{0}}}}\right|\leq\eta\quad\forall k=1,\dots,t,

(S73)

then the quantities
\{\hat{o}_{k}^{(0)}\}_{k=1}^{t}
and
\{\hat{o}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t}
computed in Steps 11–12 of Algorithm
1
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{o}_{k}^{(0)}-\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{k})}}\right|=\mathcal{O}(\eta)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{o}_{k}-\Tr(O\rho^{k})}}\right|=\mathcal{O}(\eta)\quad\forall k=1,\dots,t.

(S74)

Proof of Lemma
S10
.

We proceed by induction on
t
.

Base case (
t=1
):

From the relations
\xi_{1}^{O_{0}}=\Tr(O_{0}\rho)/2
and
\Tr(O\rho)=\Tr(O_{0}\rho)+\Tr(O)/d
,
we see that estimating
\xi_{k}^{O_{0}}
within
\eta
-additive error directly yields
\mathcal{O}(\eta)
-additive error estimates for both
\Tr(O_{0}\rho)
and
\Tr(O\rho)
. This establishes the lemma for
t=1
.

Inductive step (
t\geq 2
):

Now we assume that
t\geq 2
is a constant integer (independent of
\eta
,
O
, and
d
) and the desired result holds for all positive integers smaller than
t
.
For any permutation
\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t+1}
, we have

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes t}\otimes O_{0}}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]=\begin{cases}\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{t})&\text{when }\#(\pi)=1,\\
\vskip-11.99998pt\\
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O_{0}\rho^{\tilde{\nu}(\pi)}}}\right)\prod_{i=2}^{t}p_{i}^{\nu_{i}(\pi)}&\text{when }\#(\pi)\geq 2,\end{cases}

(S75)

where
\#(\pi)
is the number of disjoint cycles in
\pi
, and the exponents
0\leq\tilde{\nu}(\pi),\nu_{i}(\pi)<t
(for
i=2,\dots,t-1
) are some integers depending on
\pi
.
Combining this with Eq. (
5
), we derive:

\displaystyle(t+1)!\,\xi_{t}^{O_{0}}=\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t+1}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes t}\otimes O_{0}}}\right)R_{\pi}}}\right]=t!\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{t})+\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t+1},\#(\pi)\geq 2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O_{0}\rho^{\tilde{\nu}(\pi)}}}\right)\prod_{i=2}^{t}p_{i}^{\nu_{i}(\pi)}}}\right],

(S76)

where the first term uses the fact that the number of permutations in
\mathrm{S}_{t}
with
\#(\pi)=1
is
t!
.
Rearranging this relation yields:

\displaystyle\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{t})=(t+1)\,\xi_{t}^{O_{0}}-\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t+1},\#(\pi)\geq 2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O_{0}\rho^{\tilde{\nu}(\pi)}}}\right)\prod_{i=2}^{t}p_{i}^{\nu_{i}(\pi)}}}\right].

(S77)

By the induction hypothesis, if Eq. (
S73
) holds, then the estimates
\{\hat{o}_{k}^{(0)}\}_{k=1}^{t-1}
obtained from
\{\hat{p}_{k}\}_{k=2}^{t-1}
and
\{\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O_{0}}\}_{k=1}^{t-1}
satisfy

\big|\hat{o}_{k}^{(0)}-\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{k})\big|=\mathcal{O}(\eta)
.
Since the RHS of Eq. (
S77
) is a summation of a constant number of terms,
substituting these
\{\hat{o}_{k}^{(0)}\}_{k=1}^{t-1}
along with
\{\hat{p}_{k}\}_{k=2}^{t-1}
and
\hat{\xi}_{t}^{O_{0}}
into the RHS of Eq. (
S77
) produces an estimate
\hat{o}_{t}^{(0)}
for
\Tr(O_{0}\rho^{t})
within error
\mathcal{O}(\eta)
. The estimate
\hat{o}_{t}=\hat{o}_{t}^{(0)}+\Tr(O)\hat{p}_{t}/d
similarly preserves the
\mathcal{O}(\eta)
additive error for
\Tr(O\rho^{t})
.
This completes the inductive step and the proof.
∎

D
Nonlinear estimation with an approximate unitary design ensemble

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
2
in the End Matter.

Proposition S2

(Restatement of Proposition
2
)
.

Suppose
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
,
O
is an observable on
\mathcal{H}
satisfying
O\geq 0
and
\Tr(O)\geq 1
, and
t>1
is a constant integer independent of
O
and the system dimension
d
.
For any
0<\epsilon<1
, the variant CBNE protocol (given in Algorithm
S2
) can return
\epsilon
-additive error estimates for all
p_{2},\dots,p_{t}
and
\Tr(O\rho),\dots,\Tr(O\rho^{t})
with high probability, provided that:
(i)
the random unitaries used in the protocol are sampled from a
[c_{3}\epsilon^{2}\!/\!\Tr(O)^{2}]
-approximate
2(t+1)
-design ensemble,

\displaystyle{(ii)}\ N_{U}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1,\dfrac{c_{1}\Tr(O)^{2}}{d\epsilon^{2}}}}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad{(iii)}\ N_{M}\geq c_{2}\min\!\bigg\{\frac{d^{1-1/t}\Tr(O)^{2/t}}{\epsilon^{2/t}},d\bigg\},

(S78)

where
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
are some constants independent of
O
,
d
, and
\epsilon
.

Proof of Proposition
S2
.

Let
0<\delta<1
be a constant.
According to the union bound and Lemma
S13
below, in order to estimate all
p_{2},\dots,p_{t}
and

\Tr(O\rho),\dots,\Tr(O\rho^{t})
within
\epsilon
additive error and failure probability at most
\delta
, it
suffices to ensure that the following relations hold for all
k=1,2,\dots,t
:

\displaystyle(a)\ \Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{p}_{k}-p_{k}}}\right|>\frac{c\epsilon}{\Tr(O)}}}\right\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2t}\quad\text{and}\quad(b)\ \Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}-\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|>c\epsilon}}\right\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2t},

(S79)

where
0<c<1
is some constant factor independent of
O
,
\epsilon
and
d
.

According to Proposition
1
in the End Matter, the relation
(a)
in Eq. (
S79
)
holds provided that the three conditions in Proposition
S2
are satisfied.
We therefore focus on analyzing the relation
(b)
. It can be achieved if

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\big]-\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|<\frac{c\epsilon}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}-{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\big]}}\right|>\frac{c\epsilon}{2}}}\right\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2t}.

(S80)

Assume that the random unitaries used in the CBNE protocol are sampled from a

\mu
-approximate
2(t+1)
-design ensemble, where

\mu=c^{2}\delta\epsilon^{2}/[30t\Tr(O)^{2}]
.
Then Lemma
S11
below implies that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\big]-\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|=\Big|{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]-\xi_{k}^{O}\Big|\leq\mu\,\xi_{k}^{O}\leq\dfrac{c^{2}\delta\epsilon^{2}}{30t\Tr(O)^{2}}\Tr(O)\leq\frac{c\epsilon}{2},

(S81)

where the second inequality holds because
\xi_{k}^{O}\leq\Tr(O)
(see Lemma
S6
).
In addition, Chebyshev’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}-{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\big]}}\right|>\frac{c\epsilon}{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{c^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\Var\!\big[\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\big]\leq\frac{4}{c^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{N_{U}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Tr(O)^{2}\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right)+3\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\Tr(O)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}N_{U}}\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right)+\frac{2\delta}{5t},

(S82)

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
S12
below.
Therefore, to make sure that Eq. (
S80
) holds, it is sufficient to take

\displaystyle\frac{\Tr(O)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}dN_{U}}\leq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\Tr(O)^{2}d^{k-1}}{\epsilon^{2}N_{U}N_{\!M}^{k}}\leq 1,

(S83)

where we omit constant coefficients, including
\delta
and
t
.
It is straightforward to verify that these two conditions are satisfied by choosing
N_{U}
and
N_{M}
as in Eq. (
S78
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
S2
.
∎

The following two lemmas can be derived by
using arguments similar to those in the proofs of Lemmas
S4
and
S5
, respectively.

Lemma S11
.

Suppose
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
\mu
-approximate
(k+1)
-design
ensemble
\mathcal{E}_{k+1}^{\mu}
, then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\underset{U\sim\mathcal{E}_{k+1}^{\mu},\mathbf{b}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]-\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|\leq\mu\,\xi_{k}^{O}

(S84)

for any observable
O\geq 0
.

Lemma S12
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer independent of the system dimension
d
,
observable
O\geq 0
, and
U
is a random unitary sampled from a

\mu
-approximate
2(k+1)
-design ensemble
\mathcal{E}_{2k+2}^{\mu}
. Then

\displaystyle\underset{U\sim\mathcal{E}_{2k+2}^{\mu},\mathbf{b}_{U}}{\Var}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Gamma}_{k}^{U}(O)}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Tr(O)^{2}\cdot\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d},\frac{d^{k-1}}{N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right)+3\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right)^{2}.

(S85)

The following lemma can be derived by a similar induction argument used in the proof of Lemma
S10
.

Lemma S13
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer and observable
O\geq 0
.
For any
0<\eta<1
, if the estimates
\{\hat{p}_{k}\}_{k=2}^{t}
and
\{\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}\}_{k=1}^{t}
satisfy

\displaystyle|\hat{p}_{k}-p_{k}|\leq\frac{\eta}{\Tr(O)}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\xi}_{k}^{O}-\xi_{k}^{O}}}\right|\leq\eta\quad\forall k=1,\dots,t,

(S86)

then the quantities
\{\hat{o}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t}
computed in Step 11 of Algorithm
S2
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{o}_{k}-\Tr(O\rho^{k})}}\right|=\mathcal{O}(\eta)\quad\forall k=1,\dots,t.

(S87)

S4
Principal component estimation

In this section, we prove Theorem
2
in the main text,
which characterizes the efficiency of the CBNE protocol for solving the PCE task.
First, we formally define the PCE task as follows:

Definition 1

(PCE task)
.

Let
\rho
be an unknown quantum state on a
d
-dimensional Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
with largest eigenvalue
\lambda

and corresponding eigenstate
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle
.
Assume that
\rho
has a constant spectral gap, that is,
\lambda-\lambda_{2}\geq\mu
for some constant
\mu>0

independent of
d
, where
\lambda_{2}
denotes the second largest eigenvalue of
\rho
.
Given an observable
O
on
\mathcal{H}
with
\|O\|\leq 1
,
the PCE task is to estimate
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle
up to additive error
0<\epsilon<1

with high success probability.

Throughout this section, we assume that the unknown state
\rho
has the following spectral decomposition:

\displaystyle\rho=\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\lambda_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i}}}\right|,

(S88)

where the eigenvalues are arranged in decreasing order
\lambda>\lambda_{2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{d}
,
and
\lambda-\lambda_{2}\geq\mu
with constant
0<\mu\leq 1
independent of the dimension
d
.

A
Proof of Theorem
2
in the main text

To prove Theorem
2
, it suffices to prove the following theorem, which is a stronger and formal version of Theorem
2
.

Theorem 6
.

Suppose the target error
0<\epsilon<1
is a constant independent of the system dimension
d
and the target observable
O
.
Then the CBNE protocol (given in Algorithm
1
of the End Matter) can solve the PCE task in Definition
1
,
provided that:
(i)
the random unitaries are sampled from a
2(t+1)
-design ensemble,
(ii)
integer
t\geq c_{1}
,

\displaystyle(iii)\ N_{U}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1,\dfrac{c_{2}\mathfrak{B}}{d}}}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad(iv)\ N_{M}\geq c_{3}\,d^{1-1/t}\mathfrak{B}^{1/t}.

(S89)

Here
\mathfrak{B}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr(O_{0}^{2}),1}}\right\}
,
O_{0}
is the traceless part of
O
, and
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}
are some constants independent of
\mathfrak{B}
and
d
.

Since
\mathfrak{B}\leq d
,
Theorem
2
in the main text follows immediately from Theorem
6
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Let
\eta=\epsilon/2
and integer
t\geq\lceil\log_{1-\mu/3}\frac{\mu\eta}{6}\rceil
,
which, by assumption, are both constants independent of
\mathfrak{B}
and
d
.
According to Theorem
1
in the main text, the CBNE protocol can (with high probability) return estimates
\hat{o}_{t},\hat{p}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{o}_{t}-\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}}\right|\leq\frac{\mu^{t}\eta}{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{p}_{t}-\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}\right|\leq\frac{\mu^{t}\eta}{3},

(S90)

provided
N_{U}
and
N_{M}
are chosen as in Eq. (
S89
).
Furthermore, if (
S90
) holds, then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\hat{o}_{t}}{\hat{p}_{t}}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\hat{o}_{t}}{\hat{p}_{t}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\eta+\eta=\epsilon,

(S91)

that is,
\hat{o}_{t}/\hat{p}_{t}
is an
\epsilon
-additive error estimate for the desired property
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle
.
Here,
(a)
uses the triangle inequality
|x+y|\leq|x|+|y|
, and
(b)
follows from Lemmas
S15
and
S16
below.

Therefore, the CBNE protocol solves the PCE task under the three conditions of Theorem
6
.
∎

B
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma S14
.

The state
\rho
in Eq.
(
S88
)
admits the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\rho=\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}\sigma_{j},

(S92)

where

(i)

\{\sigma_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
are quantum states on
\mathcal{H}
that have mutually orthogonal supports,
i.e.,
\Tr(\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j})=0\ \forall i\neq j
.

(ii)

The coefficients
\{w_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
satisfy

w_{j}\in[\mu/3,\lambda-\mu/3]
for
j=1,2,\dots,m-1
and
w_{m}\in(0,\lambda-\mu/3]
.

(iii)

m
is an integer satisfying
0\leq m\leq 3/\mu
.

Input:
Eigenvalues
1>\lambda>\lambda_{2}\geq\lambda_{3}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{d}\geq 0
, spectral gap
0<\mu\leq\lambda
, and
\ell_{0}=1
.

1:

for

j=1,2,3,\dots
,
do

2:

\ell_{j}\leftarrow\ell_{j-1}+1

\triangleright
Start new group at
\ell_{j-1}+1

3:

while

\sum_{i=\ell_{j-1}+1}^{\ell_{j}}\lambda_{i}<\mu/3
and
\ell_{j}<d

do

4:

\ell_{j}\leftarrow\ell_{j}+1

\triangleright
Expand group until weight
\geq\mu/3

5:

end

while

6:

if

\ell_{j}=d

then

\triangleright
All eigenvalues grouped

7:

m\leftarrow j
;
break

8:

end

if

9:

if

\sum_{i=\ell_{j}+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}<\mu/3

then

\triangleright
Remaining weight too small

10:

m\leftarrow j+1
;
break

\triangleright
Assign the rest to the last group

11:

end

if

12:

end

for

Algorithm S3

Algorithm for choosing integers
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\hskip 18.49988pt

Output:

\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}
.

Proof of Lemma
S14
.

In the trivial case of
\lambda=1
, we have
\rho=\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|
and
m=0
.
So the three conditions in Lemma
S14
are automatically satisfied.

In the following, we consider the nontrivial case with
\lambda<1
.
We shall construct the decomposition of
\rho
explicitly.
Let
m\geq 1
and
\ell_{0},\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
be integers such that

1=\ell_{0}<\ell_{1}<\dots<\ell_{m}=d
.
For
j=1,2,\dots,m
, define

\displaystyle w_{j}=\sum_{i=\ell_{j-1}+1}^{\ell_{j}}\lambda_{i},\qquad\sigma_{j}=\frac{1}{w_{j}}\sum_{i=\ell_{j-1}+1}^{\ell_{j}}\lambda_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i}}}\right|.

(S93)

Since
\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\}_{i=1}^{d}
is an orthonormal basis, the states
\{\sigma_{j}\}_{j}
have mutually orthogonal supports, satisfying condition
(i)
in Lemma
S14
.
Using
\{w_{j}\}_{j}
and
\{\sigma_{j}\}_{j}
in Eq. (
S93
), the state
\rho
in Eq. (
S88
) can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\rho=\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|+\underbrace{\sum_{i=2}^{\ell_{1}}\lambda_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i}}}\right|}_{w_{1}\sigma_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=\ell_{1}+1}^{\ell_{2}}\lambda_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i}}}\right|}_{w_{2}\sigma_{2}}+\cdots+\underbrace{\sum_{i=\ell_{m-1}+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{i}}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i}}}\right|}_{w_{m}\sigma_{m}}=\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}\sigma_{j}.

(S94)

This matches the desired form in Eq. (
S92
).

To complete the proof, it remains to show that there exist integers
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}
such that condition
(ii)
is satisfied.
To this end, we give a constructive grouping of the eigenvalues, described in Algorithm
S3
.
This algorithm ensures each
w_{j}
(for
j<m
) is at least
\mu/3
by iteratively adding eigenvalues until the sum exceeds
\mu/3
.
In addition, since
\lambda_{i}\leq\lambda_{2}\leq\lambda-\mu
for
i\geq 2
, the total weight of any group
w_{j}
cannot exceed
\lambda-\mu/3
.
Algorithm
S3
terminates when
\ell_{j}=d
or the remaining eigenvalues satisfy

\sum_{i=\ell_{j}+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}<\mu/3
.
These remaining eigenvalues are grouped into
w_{m}
, which must satisfy
w_{m}\in(0,\lambda-\mu/3]
. This ensures that condition
(ii)
holds.

Finally, from the trace relation:

\displaystyle 1=\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho}}\right)=\lambda+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}\geq\lambda+(m-1)\min_{j\in\{1,2,\dots,m-1\}}w_{j}\geq\lambda+(m-1)\cdot\frac{\mu}{3},

(S95)

we derive that

\displaystyle m\leq\frac{1-\lambda}{\mu/3}+1=\frac{3-3\lambda+\mu}{\mu}\leq\frac{3}{\mu},

(S96)

where the last inequality follows because
\mu\leq\lambda<3\lambda
. This proves condition
(iii)
.

By construction, all conditions
(i)
–
(iii)
are satisfied, completing the proof of Lemma
S14
.
∎

Lemma S15
.

Suppose
0<\eta<1
, integer
t\geq\lceil\log_{1-\mu/3}\frac{\mu\eta}{6}\rceil
, and
O
is an observable with
\|O\|\leq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|\leq\eta.

(S97)

Proof of Lemma
S15
.

Lemma
S14
implies that

\displaystyle\rho^{t}=\lambda^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\sigma_{j}^{t},\quad\Tr(O\rho^{t})=\lambda^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\Tr(O\sigma_{j}^{t}),\quad\lambda^{t}\leq\Tr(\rho^{t})\leq\lambda^{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}.

(S98)

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\,\underbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\lambda^{t}}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|}_{(*)},\;\underbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\lambda^{t}+\sum_{j}w_{j}^{t}}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|}_{(**)}\,\Bigg\}.

(S99)

Next, we bound the terms
(*)
and
(**)
, respectively. First, by Eq. (
S98
) we have

\displaystyle(*)

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)}{\lambda^{t}}}}\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{w_{j}}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)}}\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{w_{j}}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\|O\|

\displaystyle\leq m\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\max_{j}w_{j}}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\frac{3}{\mu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda-\mu/3}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\leq\frac{3}{\mu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\mu}{3}}}\right)^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{3}{\mu}\cdot\frac{\mu\eta}{6}<\eta,

(S100)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
S14
and
(b)
follows from the assumption
t\geq\lceil\log_{1-\mu/3}\frac{\mu\eta}{6}\rceil
.
Second, by Eq. (
S98
) we have

\displaystyle(**)

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\lambda^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)}{\lambda^{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}{\lambda^{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|}{\lambda^{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}}\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}^{t}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\sigma_{j}^{t}}}\right)-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|}{\lambda^{t}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}2\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{w_{j}}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\leq 2m\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\max_{j}w_{j}}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{6}{\mu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda-\mu/3}{\lambda}}}\right)^{t}\leq\frac{6}{\mu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\mu}{3}}}\right)^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\frac{6}{\mu}\cdot\frac{\mu\eta}{6}=\eta,

(S101)

where
(a)
holds because
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O\sigma_{j}^{t})-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle}}\right|\leq|\!\Tr(O\sigma_{j}^{t})|+|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi}}\right|O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi}}\right\rangle|\leq 2
,

(b)
follows from Lemma
S14
,
and
(c)
follows from the assumption
t\geq\lceil\log_{1-\mu/3}\frac{\mu\eta}{6}\rceil
.
Equations (
S99
), (
B
), and (
B
) together confirm Lemma
S15
.
∎

Lemma S16
.

Suppose
0<\eta<1
, integer
t\geq 1
,
O
is an observable with
\|O\|\leq 1
,
\hat{o}_{t},\hat{p}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
,

|\hat{o}_{t}-\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)|\leq\mu^{t}\eta/3
, and
|\hat{p}_{t}-\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)|\leq\mu^{t}\eta/3
. Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\hat{o}_{t}}{\hat{p}_{t}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|\leq\eta.

(S102)

Proof of Lemma
S16
.

Let
\tilde{\eta}:=\mu^{t}\eta/3
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}\cdot\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|+1}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}}\right|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2\mu^{t}\eta/3}{\mu^{t}-\mu^{t}\eta/3}=\frac{2\eta}{3-\eta}\leq\eta,

(S103)

where
(a)
follows from the triangle inequality
|x+y|\leq|x|+|y|
;

(b)
holds because
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)/\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}\right|\leq 1
;
and
(c)
holds because
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)\geq\lambda^{t}\geq\mu^{t}
.
Using a similar argument, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}\cdot\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|+1}}\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}\leq\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}\leq\frac{2\mu^{t}\eta/3}{\mu^{t}}\leq\eta.

(S104)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\hat{o}_{t}}{\hat{p}_{t}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)-\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)\pm\tilde{\eta}}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)+\tilde{\eta}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\rho^{t}}}\right)}{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{t}}}\right)}}}\right|}}\right\}\leq\eta,

(S105)

which completes the proof.
∎

S5
Estimation of PT moments with the PTME protocol

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
3
in the main text, which characterizes the performance of our PTME protocol.
In Secs.
A
and
B
, we
derive the expectation value and variance of our estimator

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}:=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-1}\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leq N_{M}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}_{i_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{i_{k}}}}\right)\textbf{1}\big\{\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}\big\},

(S106)

respectively, where

\mathbf{b}_{U}=\big\{\hat{b}_{1},\dots,\hat{b}_{N_{M}}\big\}
and
\mathbf{r}_{U}=\{\hat{r}_{1},\dots,\hat{r}_{N_{M}}\}
denote the measurement outcomes of the PTME protocol corresponding to the random unitary
U
(see Algorithm
2
in the End Matter).
Combining these results, we then present the proof of Theorem
3
in Sec.
C
.
Throughout this section we use the relations
d=d_{A}d_{B}
,
d_{A}=d_{A_{1}}d_{A_{2}}
, and
d_{B}=d_{A_{2}}
.

A
Expectation of the estimator
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}

The statistical fluctuation of our estimator
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}
comes from:
(i) the randomness in the selection of the random unitary
U
on
\mathcal{H}_{A}
, and
(ii) the randomness in measurement outcomes
\mathbf{b}_{U}=\big\{\hat{b}_{1},\dots,\hat{b}_{N_{M}}\big\}

and
\mathbf{r}_{U}=\{\hat{r}_{1},\dots,\hat{r}_{N_{M}}\}
.
Taking both sources of randomness into account, the expectation value of
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}
is characterized by the following lemma:

Lemma S17
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer independent of the system dimension, and
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
k
-design ensemble on
\mathcal{H}_{A}
. Then

{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]=\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-1}}}\right)
, where
\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}
is defined in Eq.
(
15
)
of the End Matter.

Proof of Lemma
S17
.

Let
\rho_{U}=(U\otimes I_{B})\rho(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I_{B})
. By definition of
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}\Big|U}}\right)=\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\,\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}_{i_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{i_{k}}}}\right)\textbf{1}\big\{\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}\big\}}}\right|U}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\,\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}(-1)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\#\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\text{$-1$ among $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{r}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{r}_{i_{k}}}}\right\}$}}}\right]=\ell,\,\text{and }\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\,\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\binom{k}{\ell}(-1)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=-1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=+1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{k-\ell}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\,\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=+1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)-\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=-1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)}}\right]^{k}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}\,\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)}}\right]^{k}}}\right\},

(S107)

where in the last equality we use

\displaystyle\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=\pm 1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\frac{I_{A_{2},B}\pm\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}{2}}}\right)}}\right].

(S108)

Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)}}\right]^{k}}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I_{B})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)(U\otimes I_{B})}}\right]^{k}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\rho^{\otimes k}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I_{B})^{\otimes k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}(U\otimes I_{B})^{\otimes k}}}\right]}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\rho^{\otimes k}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{A}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{B}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}}}\right]}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes k}\tilde{H}_{b,k}}}\right),

(S109)

where
\Phi_{A}(\cdot)
is the
k
-ford twirling channel (see Eq. (
S7
)) acting on subsystem
A
, and
\tilde{H}_{b,k}
in the last line is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{b,k}

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{A}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{B}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}}}\right],

(S110)

which satisfies

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{b,k}

\displaystyle\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\sum_{\tau,\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}\mathrm{Wg}_{\pi,\tau}^{(k,d_{A})}\,(R_{\tau})_{A}\otimes\Tr_{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{A}\otimes I_{B}^{\otimes k}}}\right]}}\right\}

\displaystyle\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\sum_{\pi,\tau\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}\mathrm{Wg}_{\pi,\tau}^{(k,d_{A})}\,(R_{\tau})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}

\displaystyle\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}d_{\!A}^{\,-k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\,\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}(R_{\pi})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-1}}}\right)\sum_{\pi,\tau\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}(R_{\tau})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}}}\right].

(S111)

Here,
(a)
follows from Eq. (
S5
),
(c)
follows from Eq. (
S6
), and
(b)
holds because

\displaystyle\Tr_{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{A}\otimes I_{B}^{\otimes k}}}\right]}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}}}\right)^{\otimes k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\pi}^{\top}}}\right)_{\!A_{1}}}}\right]\Tr_{A_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)^{\otimes k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{A_{2}}\otimes I_{B}^{\otimes k}}}\right]}}\right\}=(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}.

(S112)

By combining Eqs. (
A
), (
A
), and (
A
),
the expectation of
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho^{\otimes k}(R_{\pi})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}}}\right]+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{-1}}}\right)\sum_{\pi,\tau\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho^{\otimes k}(R_{\tau})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi}^{\top})_{B}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{k}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\top_{\!B}}}}\right)^{\otimes k}(R_{\pi})_{A}\otimes(R_{\pi})_{B}}}\right]+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-1}}}\right)=\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-1}}}\right).

(S113)

This completes the proof of Lemma
S17
.
∎

B
Variance of the estimator
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}

Lemma S18
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer independent of the system dimension, and
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
2k
-design ensemble on
\mathcal{H}_{A}
. Then

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d_{A_{1}}},\frac{d^{k}}{d_{A_{1}}N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right),

(S114)

where
d=d_{A}d_{B}
.

Proof of Lemma
S18
.

The variance of
\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}
reads

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]^{2}=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right)^{2}\Big|U}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{\!-2}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}}}\right)^{\!2}\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leq N_{M}\\
1\leq j_{1}<\dots<j_{k}\leq N_{M}\end{subarray}}\,f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)-\,{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]^{2},

(S115)

where the function
f_{2}
is defined as

\displaystyle f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right):=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}_{i_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{i_{k}}\hat{r}_{j_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{j_{k}}}}\right)\mathbf{1}\big\{\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}\big\}\mathbf{1}\big\{\hat{b}_{j_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{j_{k}}\big\}\Big|U\Big].

(S116)

Let
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]
denote the number of common elements shared by
(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})
and
(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
. Then Eq. (
B
) implies that

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{\!-2}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}}}\right)^{\!2}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leq N_{M}\\
1\leq j_{1}<\dots<j_{k}\leq N_{M}\\
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l\end{subarray}}f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{\!-2}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}}}\right)^{\!2}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\binom{k+l}{k}\binom{k}{l}\,f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)\big|_{{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{\!-2}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{\!A}^{\,k}}{k!\,d_{\!A_{1}}}}}\right)^{\!2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\,\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\binom{k+l}{k}\binom{k}{l}\,\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{A_{1}}d_{B}^{\,k-l}}{d_{A}^{\,k+l}}}}\right)+\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{A_{1}}}{d_{\!A}^{\,2k}}}}\right)+\frac{d_{A_{1}}^{\,2}}{d_{\!A}^{\,2k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(k!\,\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{k-l}d_{\!A_{1}}^{\,-1}}}\right)+\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A_{1}}^{\,-1}}}\right)}}\right],

(S117)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
S19
below.
Note that

\displaystyle\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{2k}\binom{2k}{k}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-k}{N_{M}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-k-1}{N_{M}-1}}}\right)\cdots\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N_{M}-2k+1}{N_{M}-k+1}}}\right)\leq 1.

(S118)

By combining this inequality with Eq. (
B
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{N_{M}}{k}^{-2}\binom{N_{M}}{k+l}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{k-l}d_{\!A_{1}}^{\,-1}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A_{1}}^{\,-1}}}\right)}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{U}}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{k-l}}{d_{A_{1}}N_{M}^{k-l}}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A_{1}}^{\,-1}}}\right)}}\right]-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-1}}}\right)}}\right]^{2}=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{d_{A_{1}}},\frac{d^{k}}{d_{A_{1}}N_{\!M}^{k}}}}\right\}}}\right),

(S119)

where we use Lemma
S17
in
(b)
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
S18
.
∎

Lemma S19
.

Suppose
k>1
is a constant integer independent of the system dimension,
U
is a random unitary sampled from a
2k
-design ensemble on
\mathcal{H}_{A}
, integers
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\dots<i_{k}\leq N_{M}
and
1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<\dots<j_{k}\leq N_{M}
.
Let

\mathbf{b}_{U}=\big\{\hat{b}_{1},\dots,\hat{b}_{N_{M}}\big\}
and
\mathbf{r}_{U}=\{\hat{r}_{1},\dots,\hat{r}_{N_{M}}\}
denote the measurement outcomes of the PTME protocol for unitary
U
(see Algorithm
2
).
Then the function
f_{2}
defined in Eq.
(
S116
)
satisfies

\displaystyle f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{A_{1}}d_{B}^{\,k-l}}{d_{A}^{\,k+l}}}}\right)

(S120)

when
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l
with
l\in\{0,1,\dots,k-1\}
, and

\displaystyle f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d_{A_{1}}}{d_{\!A}^{\,2k}}}}\right)+\frac{d_{A_{1}}^{\,2}}{d_{\!A}^{\,2k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(k!\,\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}

(S121)

when
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=0
.

a
Proof of Eq. (
S120
) in Lemma
S19

Assume that
{\rm Co}[(i_{1},\dots,i_{k});(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})]=k-l
with
l\in\{0,1,\dots,k-1\}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)=\underset{U}{{\mathbb{E}}}\,\underset{\,\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}_{i_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{i_{k}}\hat{r}_{j_{1}}\cdots\hat{r}_{j_{k}}}}\right)\textbf{1}\big\{\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}=\hat{b}_{j_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{j_{k}}\big\}\Big|U\Big].

(S122)

Without loss of generality, we assume that
(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})
and
(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
coincide on the last

pairs.
Then

\displaystyle f_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{b}_{U},\mathbf{r}_{U},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j_{1},\dots,j_{k}}}\right\}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{\ell=0}^{2l}\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}(-1)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\#\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\text{$-1$ in $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{r}_{i_{1}},\dots,\hat{r}_{i_{l}},\hat{r}_{j_{1}},\dots,\hat{r}_{j_{l}}}}\right\}$}}}\right]=\ell,\,\text{and }\hat{b}_{i_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{i_{k}}=\hat{b}_{j_{1}}=\dots=\hat{b}_{j_{l}}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{\ell=0}^{2l}\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\binom{2l}{\ell}(-1)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=-1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=+1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{2l-\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{k-l}}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{k-l}\sum_{\ell=0}^{2l}\binom{2l}{\ell}(-1)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=-1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{\ell}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=+1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{2l-\ell}}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)^{k-l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=+1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)-\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{r}=-1,\hat{b}=b\,\Big|U}}\right)}}\right]^{2l}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=0}^{d_{A_{1}}-1}\,\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes I_{\!A_{2},B}}}\right)}}\right]^{k-l}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)}}\right]^{2l}}}\right\}}_{(*1)},

(S123)

where in the last equality we use

(S124)

The term

a

\displaystyle(*1)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{U}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I_{B})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes I_{A_{2},B}}}\right)(U\otimes I_{B})}}\right]^{k-l}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I_{B})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|b}}\right\rangle\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle b}}\right|_{A_{1}}\!\otimes\mathbb{S}_{A_{2},B}}}\right)(U\otimes I_{B})}}\right]^{2l}}}\right\}

Proof of Eq. (

\displaystyle=\frac{k!^{2}}{d_{\!A}^{\,2k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\zeta_{k}^{\mathrm{PT}}}}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d_{\!A}^{\,-2k-1}}}\right).
